The predators include lions, crocodiles, cheetahs, and hyenas. Some animals of the Serengeti are in severe danger. Rhinos are among them. Cheetahs are among them. Some antelopes are among them. Poaching is one cause. Loss of habitat caused by people is another. Take away the park and the rangers who guard the habitat. These animals would probably become extinct. Sea turtles come ashore to nest on a beach in Costa Rica. Santa Rosa National Park is part of that country's proud heritage. Costa Rica is a tiny country in Central America. It holds many treasures of nature and of culture. In Santa Rosa, many special features meet in one park. Along the Pacific coast, endangered sea turtles gather. They come by the thousands to nest. The ocean there is also excellent for surfing. Farther inland, the park has savannas.

5It also holds Central America's biggest area of tropical forest of the dry kind. That habitat supports howler monkeys. It supports wild pigs. It supports more than fifty kinds of bats. The park has many caves, and bats make nests in them. Scientists come to this rich landscape to study a meeting of worlds. They study how forest life and ocean life affect each other. Two historic places keep the memory of important battles. Those battles were part of Costa Rica's fight to become free of Spain. Visitors can tour the sites. They can hike, camp, and surf. Many bats nest in the caves of Santa Rosa. Land close by is protected. The protection keeps a habitat for the bats. It also keeps habitat for the other wild animals of the area. Rock Creek Park sits among the people of a city. It is in Washington, D.C., in the U.S.A. It is easy to picture a national park as a wide wild region. A major city seems an odd place for a park. That picture is harder to form. Rock Creek Park is exactly that. It is a small area of 1,700 acres. It preserves cultural heritage and natural heritage. It lies in the center of the capital city of the United States. The park was set up in 1890. It gives people who live in the city a piece of nature. They can slip away among plants and animals.

6They can stand by the rushing water of a forest. A theater holds outdoor concerts in summer. It holds plays then too. The park is full of historical artifacts. Some date from when the capital of the United States was founded. Some come from the Civil War. Some come from the Piscataway people, who were displaced. From Rock Creek Park, you can see the Washington Monument. Other city escapes in the United States include four sites. The first marks where Theodore Roosevelt was born. It is a National Historic Site in New York City. The second is Gateway National Recreation Area, also in New York City. The third is the Jefferson National Expansion Memorial, in St. Louis, Missouri. The fourth is Golden Gate National Recreation Area, in San Francisco, California. Visitors tour a marine conservation area in Quebec. That place is named for the Saguenay and the St. Lawrence. Canada lists it as a National Marine Conservation Area. It is in Quebec, Canada. Mountains climb from the shores of a fjord called the Saguenay. Say it like this: SAG-eh-nay fee-YORD. The fjord is a narrow arm of the sea. It is extremely deep. From the east, water from the estuary of the St. Lawrence runs into the fjord. An estuary is a meeting place. River water that is fresh mixes in that place with ocean water that is salty.

7Waters in this fjord do not all share one temperature. They stack in layers. Waters of different saltiness form layers too. Wildlife in one layer is not the same as wildlife in another layer. This unusual mix of water is the whole park. The park is entirely under water. Fresh water and salt water mix in the fjord. The mix supplies many nutrients. All sorts of sea creatures come for those nutrients. Plankton live here. Fish, birds, and mammals live here too. Many kinds of whales arrive in the warmer months. They include minke whales, fin whales, and pilot whales. They include humpback whales and blue whales. The beluga is rare. It is also called the white whale. It lives here all year. Seals are abundant. Fish are abundant. Crabs are plentiful. Shellfish are plentiful too, and shellfish are invertebrates. How do you visit a park that is under water? Many people choose a boat. The boat lets them get near whales and other animals. The park offers trips to watch whales. It offers trips in kayaks. A kayak is a small boat, something like a canoe. Visitors can also scuba dive. They can hike. They can go ice fishing. Many whales come to Saguenay Fjord while the months are warmer. Other one-of-a-kind places stand around the world. Volcanoes National Park is in Hawai'i, in the U.S.A. The park has the world's largest volcano.

8It is also the world's most active volcano. Visitors watch lava that is red-hot and glowing. The lava rolls in fiery streams out of the volcano. It falls into the ocean and sends up plumes of steam. Up close, this is the island being made. Wild areas in the park offer hiking and camping. Hikers move among landscapes and plants found in Hawai'i and not in many other places. Steam rises where lava meets the ocean. Everglades National Park is in Florida, in the U.S.A. It is the largest subtropical wilderness in North America. It sits on the homelands of the Seminole, the Taino, and the Tequesta people. Fragile swamps are preserved here. Grasslands are preserved. A marshy coastline is preserved. Rare mammals live here too. One is the Florida panther. Another is the manatee. A photo shows Florida's everglades. Sareks National Park is in Sweden. Sweden was the first country in Europe to create national parks. The Arctic Circle lies south of this park. The park sits in the far north. It is the largest wild area in Western Europe. Majestic mountains fill it. Glaciers fill it too. The Saami have this park as an ancestral home as well. The Saami are also called the Lapp people. They herd reindeer. In the park called Sarek, reindeer graze. Tassili N'Ajjer National Park is in Algeria. Say the name like this: tah-SEE-LEE n-AH-jer. Algeria is in Northern Africa.

9It sits in the fierce Sahara. The land was not always desert. People of past civilizations left art behind. The art includes drawings. It includes engravings. Together they number more than 15,000. They are in caves. They are on the sides of cliffs. The art records human history since 6000 bce. In Algeria, one painting on rock tells a story. A park can already be official and still face many threats on the land it saves. In some countries, people grow crops on park land. They graze livestock there. They gather firewood there, so they can warm themselves and feed hungry people. Wars can damage these fragile environments. Unstable government can do harm too. That harm comes when parties fight with violence. Some protected lands hold resources such as oil fields. They may hold mineral resources or forests. Some people believe those resources should be used so the economy can grow. People sometimes question where a national park should end. They are sometimes changed. People bargain over who has power over the resources. Yemen has a nature sanctuary on Socotra Island. Trash still lies across one lovely beach there. The geysers of Yellowstone National Park draw millions of visitors every year. A park can be well protected and still suffer if too many people come. Visitors may leave litter. They may trample trails. Snowmobile exhaust may dirty the air. It can also be hard to feel the wild when people surround you.

10National parks give endangered wildlife a refuge. They protect millions of kinds of plants. Those plants give us oxygen. They also help bring down the temperature of the atmosphere. People keep destroying natural ecosystems. They do it to build roads and buildings. Protecting these beautiful places is more vital than ever. Later generations should be able to enjoy these wild spaces. Indigenous peoples need to be partners in that work. Governments in countries across the world must join them. Together they can preserve the land. These words have special meanings in this book. Artifacts are objects, and pieces of art, that come from ancient times. Diversity means many different kinds of things living together. An ecosystem joins a community of living things with the home around them. Endangered means the whole group could disappear. An estuary is where a river finishes. Fresh water mixes with salty water there. Extinct means no longer in existence, fully wiped out. A fjord is a narrow inlet of the sea between steep slopes and cliffs. A habitat is the natural home of an animal or a plant. Migrate means a yearly move. The move goes from one area into a different area. Poaching means killing a wild animal in a way that is against the law. Preserved means kept safe from harm. Savannas are grasslands that are dry.
